ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2809	Please, Mr. Minister.

However, I am rather skeptical about this proposal, I must say quite frankly to you, because this proposal for a law is rather difficult. It is a very large authority of 26 members, and it is, as you would like to set up, a mixture of research, control and advisory bodies. In my experience, it does not promise a real impulse for consumer protection in this design. You know that I am very much concerned about consumer protection. I believe that Austria needs consumer protection, which is free from the social partners and also free from any influence by the state that is independent. For such a goal I fight very much, and for that I strive.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2851	Additional question? Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2866	Federal Minister! According to the Data Protection Act, a Data Protection Council has the same task as the Consumer Protection Council we are planning. What are you – and this is my question – about the activity of the Data Protection Council, which supports the Federal Government on very essential issues of data protection rights?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2882	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2897	This is an existing facility, which I naturally have a positive attitude towards. But we must not confuse the Data Protection Council and the data protection problem with a Consumer Protection Council and consumer protection issues. Data protection concerns very difficult legal issues, in particular fundamental rights issues, the field of tension between fundamental rights and other claims raised in this field, which require a different solution capacity from consumer protection issues. In the field of consumer protection, it is about independence, creativity in everyday life and also inconsistency with those who threaten consumer protection on a daily basis. And that's exactly the structure I've described.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2915	Additional question? – Mrs Achatz, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2931	Federal Minister! In which areas Are you moving ahead with consumer protection in practice in the near future?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2946	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e2961	In particular, we will review home contracts, i.e. those contracts, which are concluded by nursing homes in particular with seniors and which often include loads for the benefit of the economically overpowered one, namely the home. For example, domestic residents can get a caretaker into their room against their will, can be moved, the conditions can be changed and so on. We will also deal with the power supply contracts, i.e. the terms and conditions on which some of them have the same imbalance. They're little transparent. There are also problems in connection costs, as we know. In addition, we will also deal with misleading competitions because they put economic pressure on the population. Here, as I have described earlier, we must be able to act spontaneously, unattendedly and without burden from third-party access.

My knowledge comes this week a decision by the competent court in Upper Austria, which we expect to bring to Austria a new impetus for the intangible right of compensation, i.e. the advocacy of immaterial damage. We have a working group in the ministry, and our current objective is to make the intangible compensation, if the need arises – and we expect that – to integrate in the area of the ABGB and not to make a new law.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e4401	Please have a reply, Minister of State.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e5877	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e5892	Deputies! Sometimes, with the German language, you can't express everything as with the English. So I say it in English: Enlargement is enrichment. – This concerns in particular the environmental impact. The application of European environmental standards will lead to enormous improvements in the air sector. This is particularly crucial in the context of cross-border pollution transfers. The application of both the Water Framework Directive and the water protection standards and the waste management standards will lead to a massive improvement over a longer period of time, because the investments are huge and cannot be made from today to tomorrow. We achieve positive effects, in particular, by avoiding environmental dumping in a larger economic area, by increasing the number of members of Europe's environment and by increasing biodiversity, because there are still many areas in the candidate countries that are considered to be withdrawal areas for many species and are used to preserve biodiversity. Overall, I therefore believe that there are many economic opportunities for Austrian companies in eco-technology in these countries. There is an area to be seen with concern, namely transport development, which is also compensated for by the application of environmental standards in motor vehicle technology and fuel technology, so that we can expect, for example, a reduction of up to 40 percent in the ozone sector.

I also explained my position on this. I believe that it is very crucial that we ensure the possibility of production without this technology for organic farming in the future.

In recent years, the Social Democrats have had the opportunity to facilitate and change in this respect. Any worker who has so far spent more time caring for a member of his family association than his or her free time, or has accompanied a dying person, has so far threatened his or her job. In the future, a worker with family care has a right to reduce normal working time, to change normal working hours or to an exemption from the loss of work for up to three months with an extension of up to half a year. During this period, these persons are insured with unemployment, sickness and pension. This is particularly important for women who often have difficulties in acquiring the corresponding pension insurance periods. This is guaranteed. ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e10303	We'll get to the 2nd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mr Brosz. His speaking time is gladly set to 6 minutes. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e10330	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! The discussion on the application concerning the support of the candidacy of the city of Salzburg and the federal state of Tyrol for the 2010 Winter Olympics was marked in advance by an attempt to reach a four-party agreement here. Unfortunately, this has not been achieved. First of all, I would like to say that the Green Group will also make clear its support here. This will be documented by bringing in our own motion for a resolution, which is relatively extensive and will therefore be distributed with the agreement of the President. – However, there are clear differences between these two applications. [[President Dr Fassleven takes over the chairmanship.]] Let me say something about the procedure: For our part, there has been a very honest effort – and I would also in any case admit to Mr Maier of the Socialist Group – to come here to a joint request. I was somewhat surprised yesterday, however, when Mr Grollitsch told me that we could not negotiate the text at all, because Mrs Vice-Chancellor would not be able to do so and therefore that what we have been presented was to be carried along in such a way, in a modified form, but in one that we could never actually talk about four parties. [[Abg. Dr. Grollitsch: Stay with the truth, my colleague!]] – That is the truth, and this truth is probably not different from what it is said here. If one submits a parliamentary initiative proposal and leads four parties to it, then it should be possible to reach an agreement here in the House on this. Unfortunately, it was no longer possible yesterday to reach an agreement on parts which in themselves would not have been so far apart. What we have criticised from the outset in this form of the present proposal was the fact that from our point of view the framework conditions were not sufficiently clarified. We would have liked some points to be explicitly named. I do not know whether you will stick to the original request or to the one you have revised. The latter is, in any case, much better than what was initially introduced, because progress has been made in some areas that are important to us. We have said that, in principle, if the criteria of financial responsibility, the criteria of ecological responsibility and also the criterion of a barrier-friendly and barrier-free design of venues and venues were guaranteed, we have tried to retain that in a request. In that application you have submitted, a flat-rate support for the Olympic Games is formulated, but it does not contain any detailed information on these areas. We shall therefore put forward our own motion for a resolution, which I shall now explain in its basic texts. The proposal contains eight points, and I will say on the subject what our most important aspects would be. The first three points of our request refer to conditions required by the IOC by the governments concerned in order to make support possible at all. There are no objections from our side. The fourth point contains the demand for a commitment by the federal government to a third-party financing of the sports facilities, together with a clear presentation of re-use concepts. Our concern was to be able to discuss these here in Parliament too. It is not true that Olympic games represent only a chance, as is true in the application, but that also involves various risks. Among other things, the question should be clarified as to what happens to the issuing sites afterwards, whether there are possibilities to use them accordingly. If it goes badly, then, as it were, ruins can be left, which will hardly be used afterwards. Reuse concepts must therefore be drawn up. The fifth point concerns transport and technology. That is what we were talking about, clearly stating that we want a clear priority for public transport and public transport. In the area of rail transport in particular, we see a central point that needs to be taken into account. Indeed, it is to be feared that, as we have often experienced in large projects, it is being tried to deal largely with public transport, by public transport, but that the opportunity to really improve public transport is denied, for example in the form of rail projects, in the form of rail development. Therefore, we expressly call for the preference of the projects from the general transport plan, for example as regards the extension of the railway line Vienna – Salzburg. All of this would be necessary in order to really come to a solution that is also environmentally compatible. The sixth point relates to the question – and that is what the whole thing I believe has failed to do in the end – how environmental experts are involved. There was consensus that they should be included. But what was not there was a consensus on what importance they should have. Although the inclusion of experts alone is loyal, it does not have to mean anything, as we have seen in many areas. We wanted to make it clear that what is being developed jointly by the experts, including with the national environmental departments, must be implemented. This was the central point and it was not possible to reach an agreement and include it in the request. Paragraph 7 calls on the Federal Government to draw up a financial risk assessment and a cost-benefit analysis for the winter games and report to the National Council. This relates once again to the fact that there are opportunities and risks there. Point 8 contains the demand for a clear commitment to design the 2010 Winter Olympics in such a way that they are accessible to disabled people, especially in view of the fact that the Paralympics are to be performed immediately after the Winter Olympics, and also in Salzburg. This is not only about bringing disabled people to the venues – my colleague Haidlmayr has made it very clear – but also about the possibility of achieving sustainable and lasting improvement in the field of public transport – especially in Salzburg, there are more problems than in other capitals. Finally, I would like to say: We would have been willing to make a joint request. Our motion for a resolution also supports the application of the city of Salzburg and the federal states of Salzburg and Tyrol for the 2010 Winter Olympics. However, in the undetermined form in which this proposal has been tabled, it was not possible for us to agree to a four-party agreement here.

Maier, Dr. Grollitsch, Lexer, Schasching and colleagues concerning the application of the city of Salzburg for the 2010 Winter Olympics The National Council wants to decide: The resolution annexed to the Committee's report 1120 of the Supplements with the text "The Federal Government is asked to support the application of the city of Salzburg together with the federal states of Salzburg and Tyrol in order to support the 2010 Winter Olympics in order to ensure a successful application phase until the international decision on the 2nd. In July 2003, the following paragraphs shall be added: This support shall include, in particular, the necessary safety measures, the participation in complying with environmental objectives and criteria, the preparation of an optimal transport concept (with particular attention being paid to public transport) as an integral part of the general transport plan and the co-financing of the necessary sports infrastructure on the basis of appropriate re-use concepts for those sports venues. On the basis of a comprehensive application plan, an overall cost assessment and an assessment of the economic effects of the 2010 Winter Olympics should be drawn up on the part of the applicant city of Salzburg and the federal states of Salzburg and Tyrol. But I do not hide that I welcome the fact that you say a basic yes to this application, although with other accesses. The positive sporting and interconnecting dimensions of the Olympic Games are generally recognized and are in the foreground. Nevertheless, one should not lose sight of the economic component of such major events and deal with them accordingly. The application for Olympic Winter Games alone brings massive image gains for the regions concerned, even for the whole of Austria, and thus a sustainable economic success. But I say clearly: We can only do this if we move in the same direction together in the sense of the Olympic thought. The comparison makes us sure! I can tell you a few numbers of the last Winter Olympics in Salt Lake City: 78 competitions, 3 500 athletes and maintainers. Ten 000 media reporters have written their fingers every day, and have reported on this event via electronic media throughout the world. A million on-site spectators, at the sports venues, have provided enthusiasm for the competitions, and more than a billion people have brought these Olympic games to the well-tempered living room via TV, via electronic media, and have been enthusiastic about it. The advertising value of this media work is practically priceless, and it would therefore be grossly negligent if the Republic of Austria, if Salzburg and Tyrol, if the regions concerned did not take this opportunity to reach similar for our home country. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We should therefore, together with all our strength, strive to bring the 2010 Winter Olympics to Austria. We want to align simple but charming games with heart, without any financial experiment, with a clear rejection of gigantomania and protrusion à la Hollywood. We want to conquer not only the hearts of athletes, but also all those people who participate in these Olympic games on site or via media, for the beauty of our country, for the cultural diversity of our regions and for the hospitality and for the warmth of our citizens, so that they can also come to Austria as tourists. Of course, we want to put a gentle approach to the environment in the foreground at these Olympic Games, but we have it relatively easy because most of the sports venues for alpine and Nordic competitions are already present and there will be no sustainable intervention in nature. We want to create 2 000 new jobs in the short term with these Olympic Games, to secure and create 500 new jobs of a qualified kind in the long term. This is a rather ambitious project in a time of difficult labour market situations. There are, of course, there and there, as with all major events, hesitaters, Zauders, doubters. We should really take these people's concerns seriously, we should deal with them and, at the same time, try together to convince these people of the sense of purpose, of the sustainable economic impact of these Olympic Games. There's also the statement that even the application makes no sense because we don't have a chance anyway. All these twins, hesitants and Zauders I call and invite them to act together in the spirit of the Olympic oath that the athletes make at the Special Olympics: Let's win, and if we can't win, let's at least try bravely! Together we can do it!

It was therefore important for us, and this was also a request from the federal government that we set up a controlling advisory board, which is devoted to the many financial issues. By the way, we have been using this procedure, since I was responsible for this department, in all major infrastructure projects, in all the projects that we support with the funding of federal sports funding. For example, we did this at the Salzburg Stadium – with a success for all those involved, as I believe – where we were able to minimize the costs by much more careful planning. The applicant company was in my ministry two days ago and presented the concept for 2010 and thus also gave us the start for a top application. I can only ask all parties involved to participate with the necessary commitment and with great competence so that we can realize this great opportunity for Austria. On the basis of this concept, which is now available to us, we will also examine and decide the level of participation of the federal and other regional authorities. This is a whole series of projects which are not only concerned with my area, but also about infrastructure projects to be discussed with the Ministry of Infrastructure. But also the Ministry of Economic Affairs and other departments are of course involved in close cooperation here. I assume that differences and personal interests and disputes, which have been singled out in recent weeks, will now be in the background and all parties involved will also be fully committed to an optimal application. Such an Olympic application is not a party policy issue, it is a matter for Austria as a whole, and that is why we must all join forces together. [[It is a pleasure for the liberals and for representatives of the ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Austria – and I would like to underline this in its importance – if we were to be a venue for the 2010 Winter Olympics, we would also be hosts for the Paralympic Games. We can also show the importance of disabled sport in Austria, and we have also stressed this very intensively in the last two years: We have awarded awards for the athletes, for the medal winners of Sydney and Salt Lake City at the Paralympic Games, at an altitude in which there has never been in Austria, because I believe that even these athletes have to perform sports excellence and excellence. Therefore also the involvement in "Top Sport Austria" and for the first time also the possibility for disabled athletes to use "Top Sport" funds, top sports promotions. We are now running a rubble campaign in favour of the sport of disabled persons, which Franz Klammer is promoting and which will also bring about appropriate means for the sport of disabled persons. We have sports-scientific support for disabled athletes through the IMSB, and I also very much hope that the BSO will fulfill its commitment – and this is a call we have been making for many years – not only as a member of the IMSB, but also as a full-fledged, equal member with funding from the BSO. I very much hope that this will be implemented in this year. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The financial equipment of the sport has been addressed before. I would like to point out that, of course, in the preparation of these Olympic Games, we also focus on sporty preparation, just as we support and finance sporty preparation for this major event together with the Challenge 2008 project. As far as the Lotto-Toto funds are concerned, it is right that the legal basis expires at the end of this year and that this will, of course, be a very crucial point in the negotiations with the Ministry of Finance. I would just like to point out that we may also have to go new ways here, because, a few years ago, the BSO would have agreed to the offer of the then Federal Chancellor of Climate Change, who offered not to set upper and lower limits for Lotto-Toto funds, but to set a percentage, then in the last three years, the sport would have been able to benefit more from these funds by 200 million schilling than by the current regulation. I think we have to make ourselves aware that it is not so easy that we just need to say, we now increase this amount by the sum x and distribute it as before with the casting can, but if we want to put impulses in sports, then we have to set priorities. My aim, therefore, will also be to find additional resources, but to focus on young people's work, for professional trainer training in this area, in order to ensure the efficiency of the use of resources. I very much regret that the associations were not very cooperative, which affected the insight into the conduct, so that it can also be found whether these funds have been used effectively in the past. I therefore recognise myself clearly and clearly to more resources for sport, but I also say that in my opinion the system of casting can not be the right thing, but that we will also link priorities and goals with these means in order to support the training of young people, the training of trainers, the improved work of young people in this area accordingly. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] With such an application, of course, we also have the opportunity to postpone the sports facilities, i.e. to make appropriate investments in the sports infrastructure sector. For example, if you knew Barcelona in front of the Olympic Games and looked at this city afterwards, you know what can be achieved in a city with investments in this area. We also want to create appropriate modern training and competition sites. But I do not believe that we can see this in isolation from the sports policy objectives. If one considers that in the last ten to 15 years we have built ice cream halls by many million schillings throughout Austria, but at the same time we have failed to develop an Austrian ice-cream scene at top level, then it must be said that we can hardly explain these investments to the citizens. In other words, we must also focus on all sports venue infrastructure investments and combine sport policy objectives with them. For example, we now have a very interesting project in the matter of tobogganing, in discussion, namely a Federal Research Centre in Innsbruck. Markus Prock, our toboggan-olimpia medal winner, has had this idea. I believe that we must take note that we will not be able to do anything with the most beautiful sports venues without a decisive young work in this area if we no longer have the athletes who can also successfully pursue their competition. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The idea of fairness is at the heart of the sport, especially at the Olympic Games. That is why it is also important to mention that doping deeply contradicts this idea and that we all share the responsibility to link strict doping criteria and controls to competitions at major sporting events as well as at Olympic Games. We have just, I believe, sent a Federal Sports Promotion Act in review two weeks ago, in which the anti-doping provisions of the European Anti-Doping Convention are legally enshrined. This means that in the event of a breach of these provisions, subsidies must also be suspended and repaid. We will set up an §-8 Commission, which will be composed of representatives of my department from each representative of the Ministry of Social Affairs, the Ministry of Finance, the BMVIT and the Ministry of the Interior. The Commission will also start its work soon. This is the second step after the amendment of the Medicines Act and the reform in this area, according to which doping infringements not only involve administrative penalties, but also criminal penalties. I believe that this is actually necessary in the interests of sport and that we have therefore created the necessary legal conditions for fair competition in this area. We must also recognise that sport has a special importance in the prevention of drugs for young people and children. This is a problem with which all of us have been confronted more recently. Sport is therefore a crucial part here. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have just started a pilot project after two representatives of the Kolping Drug Advisory Centre were with us and told us about the successes they had achieved in the withdrawal of former drug addicts by giving them a new sense of life and motivation. Within the framework of this pilot project, these sports activities will be promoted and will ensure that young people can use the Federal Sports and Leisure Centres for a training camp, that there are Schnupper courses for karate, judo and other sports, that this project is cared for by professional trainers and that new life prospects for young people are also created. In addition, I would like to ask this House for support on this issue, how we can implement drug prevention and alcohol prevention in young people with sport, to start with NGOs, government agencies and parliamentarians, to whom I cordially invite and for whom I also ask for the necessary support in this area. I believe that, in this sense, we should see the 2010 Winter Olympics not only from an economic and sporting point of view, but from the point of view of the overall significance for Austria, and that, as Mr Böhacker said, we should all work together if everyone is really behind this project and if we know the competitors, we have realistic and very good opportunities to bring these Olympic Games to Austria.

It also mentioned the importance of major events also for the economic environment around these events, but also far beyond. Ladies and gentlemen, you know that in addition to the application for the 2010 Winter Olympics in Salzburg and Kitzbühel, Austria is also applying for the European Football Championship together with Switzerland for 2008. The Chamber of Commerce, together with the Austrian Football Federation, has conducted an investigation into the impact of such an event on jobs, the market, the economy and the economic environment. These figures are quite impressive, and I believe they are, if not one, transferable on the effects of the Olympic Games. The example of this study for the 2008 football championship shows that, for example, this event alone created 6 600 additional jobs in Austria: 2 200 in the construction industry and 4 400 in tourism. However, this would also result in effects for the advertising industry, amounting to around € 100 million. From this title, tax revenue would also rise by around €50 million, and investment plus tourism expenditure should also be included in this context. I do not want to keep a sentence from this study, in which the author of this study says that, above all, the image improvement of the tourism destination in Austria would experience an economic impulse that would go far beyond 2008 – based on the Olympic Games: far beyond 2010. All these arguments are that it is not only – even if it should be in the foreground – that it is a sporting event, but it is also very likely that this event would be of great importance to our country, to the economy of our country, to the image of our country. I believe that it is also worth considering for all of us that we are committed to these events and to the applications for their presentation in our country. Mrs. Vice chancellor! I would also like to thank you very much for your special commitment to these two major events and for their application, together with the national associations. I think it would be good and right – and I am looking forward to that being done – that at least the three large groups of this House are making a clear commitment to this event with this motion for a resolution, in this case at the Salzburg/Kitzbühel Olympics in 2010. I thank you very much for that now. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e18455	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Mr. State Secretary! Most of the EU's people suffer from an infectious disease at least once in their lifetime. Transmissible diseases do not make at state borders Hold and can spread quickly if they are not fought. I quote from the EU epidemiology report: "The mobility of the population, migration and globalisation as well as environmental changes favour the rapid spread of diseases that have rarely occurred in the EU." Protection vaccinations, ladies and gentlemen, are among the most important preventive measures available in medicine. Usually, all vaccines are protected from a disease. In addition, diseases that are transmitted from human to human beings, such as children’s paralysis, hepatitis, measles, coughing cough, can be eliminated during continued and regular vaccination. It is particularly important, however, that the vaccination begins already in the infant age as a basic immunisation. Vaccine complications are not useful in any relation resulting from the Gar-non-infection of a basic immunisation, i.e. a vaccination in the infant age. In contrast to this, the re-flaking of epidemics often poses major risks worldwide, such as the smallpox, tuberculosis – an increase particularly in the Eastern states – or the children’s paralysis that has not yet been eradicated. The aim of the WHO programme to completely eradicate child paralysis in 2002 could not be fully achieved, but after all, the number of diseases has fallen from 2 599 in 2000 to less than 500 in 2001. A further vaccination in this area is therefore essential. Not so long yet, but even safer is now vaccinated with the polytot vaccine IPV, which is used for this purpose. The advantage of this vaccine against the old vaccine is that it is used in children and implies a six-fold vaccination. That is, children can be vaccinated against diphtheria, tetanus, coughs, flu, hepatitis and child paralysis. This law or the omission of an obsolete law is an initiative against the flood of law. In other words, we do something that is no longer necessary, and we are turning to an important and new innovation especially in the vaccinations. I can't imagine someone can't agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e19346	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! In the context of this item on the agenda, I would like to dedicate myself to the amendments to the law of midwives. The previous law of midwives no longer met the needs of practice in some respects. Especially in the case of domestic births or in the case of domestic care immediately after a childbirth, the supply of drugs for the midwives was so far difficult. With the present legislative amendment, the midwives are now given the opportunity to obtain the medicines necessary for possible complications at or immediately after birth directly from the pharmacy and to take these medicines in stock. This change has been agreed with the midwives and medical representatives and is an important measure to support the practice of midwives in cooperation with the doctors. The Government template also includes the rules for the collection of the committee contribution. In the case of midwives in a service relationship, this contribution will be taken from the respective service provider in the future. Finally, the agreement has been implemented with Switzerland, which states that the entire training of midwives in Austria – all diplomas, certificates of examination, qualifications and so on – is also recognised in Switzerland, and vice versa, the training of Swiss midwives is also recognised in Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! This change to the law of midwives is appropriate, practical and supports our midwives, who have especially dedicated themselves to protecting the life of mother and child around birth. It has also been possible to contribute to the prevention of birth complications by means of meaningful measures, without compromising the core competence of the doctors.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e19993	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! I would like to say two short sentences at the beginning of my speech on this novel on the genetic engineering law and then continue with the general debate on the issue of release in Austria. This amendment to the Genetic Engineering Act is essentially aimed at implementing EU law. These are deregulation provisions and faster and more flexible procedures to simplify procedures to strengthen the economic location. We see this quite differently, we see that in individual cases also positive, but there are some points in it which I have great concerns about and which have ultimately been crucial to the fact that we cannot accept this novel, as it is now present to us. I think that in the case of such a risky technology as genetic technology, it is quite appropriate to make the procedures as transparent as possible to guarantee as much public participation as possible. That is why I regret that the hearing has de facto been abolished in closed-system procedures. I would have liked this to be maintained to this extent. There are also substantial deregulation steps in this novel, measures which I cannot understand and which have also been massively criticised in the review process, such as a very large-scale regulatory authorisation, which completely exempts certain works from the provisions of the Genetics Act. I don't see that, I think that's an unnecessary deregulation, because you should be careful at first with such a technology. With the regulation on the treatment issue, somatic gene therapies, I am not quite happy either, I do not think that this has been completely excluded from the provisions of Sections II and III. In my opinion, however, the liability arrangements also need to be revised where there are some shortcomings. In general, you can say: This law has almost been passed word-identically in essential areas, except for a single opinion, by the opinion of industry. There is a term that is "negligible risk". I think that this formulation is not good and it is a failure. [[Abg. May. Kogler: This is a threat!]] I wonder, What is a negligible risk? Is that a small risk, is that a low risk or is that even a risk? What is that? – I mean, you shouldn't work with such a technology. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] What I don't understand in this novel – well, it was de facto forced, because there is already a great delay in implementation in Austrian law – is the following: Why has the amendment of the Genetic Engineering Act not been taken as an opportunity to draw up a comprehensive novel that resolves the problems that have been encountered in the area of accidental releases, especially last year, and which closes the massive legal gaps? The problem does not exist until yesterday or since the last week, but we had it last year in the destruction of seeds, in the questions of who is responsible and how we can even intervene with the so-called genetically contaminated maize. It has not been taken into account in any way. I believe that it is actually a sign of poverty that one negates problems for so long and that its solution is being pushed to the long bank. Federal Minister! I would have liked to see in this novel the gap of law closed to all, with the following questions: Who is responsible for accidental releases? Is the genetic engineering law applicable at all? Who has to pay the compensation? – It is important to ensure that one can get through very quickly in such cases. There's no guarantee that this is not happening again. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Second point: We now face a very problematic situation with a moratorium in Austria and a moratorium at European level. In Austria, five years ago, there was genetic engineering popular desire, where there was a very strong vote of the Austrian population for not wanting genetic technology in agriculture and in food production. At European level, there has now been a voluntary ban on release permits for four years. The Austrian industry has also joined this. The Austrian food production industry has also taken a very good step with the food code by also making non-genotechnic production legally binding. All these preparatory work, all these efforts could come to an end this year, and this creates a very serious situation. The moratorium at European level will definitely fall this year, and if we do not express a strong political will that we have failed to apply genetic engineering in Austrian agriculture and food production, then next year we guaranteed genetically modified seeds in our fields. I believe that this is very difficult for three reasons: New studies are now again and again, which clearly describe the economic futility or the economic risk of the use of genetic engineering, especially in the vicinity of organic farming. Such studies are available both from the European Environment Agency and from the Austrian side, namely the Upper Austrian study, which shows very clearly that this philosophy of adversarial cannot actually grasp each other. It is not quite easy to implement legally, but I and my whole group firmly believe that a GMO-free agriculture must at least be guaranteed in Austria and that there are ways to do so. However, this is a condition for political will. I see this political will with three parties in this House, including the Liberals, but I do not see it at the Austrian People's Party! Ladies and gentlemen of the ÖVP, I call on you to give up your opposition to a concept that 80 percent of the Austrian population and three-quarters of the members of the National Council actually want to give up and contribute to the fact that we come to a consensus here that we are establishing Austria as a genetically-technology-free agricultural zone and that we can also reap economic benefits from it. I do not think I need to mention that 700 municipalities, that many mayors, that environmental organisations, that several days of land – whether that is Burgenland, Carinthia or Upper Austria – have a massive interest in it. I do not understand at all how one can still argue that one does not want to follow such a concept. With its resistance, the ÖVP is completely isolated, and I strongly appeal to them to reconsider their attitude to this. If Minister Molterer asks what a GMO-free zone is at all, then I recommend him to contact Commissioner Fischler, who has done this very precisely and who now also represents the philosophy that the co-existence of organic farming and genetic engineering in agriculture is problematic, that at least there must be a guarantee that there are large GMO-free zones. [[Abg. May. Kogler: The ÖVP flees from responsibility!]] The urgent need for the Austrian population after the establishment of a non-genotechnic zone must be taken into account. The proposal, which is being adopted here today, is a first step in this direction, but it is far from enough. We Greens will not stop pursuing this concept, nor will we be prevented from continuing to debate it in this House.

What do the farmers want? – We clearly see that too. Greenpeace has conducted a survey – not among the organic farmers – among the conventional. And what happened? – Farmers want non-genotechnic seeds. Of course! They too want good quality. They want to have such products in their fields, which are then also desired by consumers. . I do not understand the ÖVP on this point at all, Minister of State. What happens on the market? - That's actually the excitement. That is, free of genetic engineering within the meaning of the Austrian food code, Mr. Federal Minister. You know, it's also about operating resources. For example, in such dairy farms, not even genetically modified soya must be fed. So these are the developments on the market, ladies and gentlemen. I am very sure that modern medicine and the modern fight against viral and bacterial diseases and diseases that occur in areas that are still well below this level will be given a new opportunity with the methods of genetic engineering if they are applied positively in the interests of humans. I therefore believe that we have made an important first step today. My position is that in Austria, at least the recognized international national parks with their highly sensitive ecological systems should remain per futuro free of genetic engineering zones, similar to those of the Italians with the Toscana or the United Kingdom, a step which, although these areas will not be 100% free of genetic engineering, will at least try to keep them free from genetic engineering under European laws. I believe that we should also make efforts in Austria and that my house should support this. However, I also need the support of the federal states and the national leaders for this because, according to the constitutional situation of our Republic, nature and landscape protection is clearly a matter of the countries. At best I cannot rule against better knowledge and conscience over the Constitution, and I will not. This petition deals specifically with the stop of the Zistersdorf–Drösing branch railway. ==References====External links== ==References== This petition then lists a whole series of negative things: that no replacement traffic has been used, that there are problems in student transport and long waiting times et cetera. So there are examples of negatives. We have this petition like every petition in the Committee meeting of 10. In October 2001, the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology agreed that opinions should be obtained from the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology and also from the Office of the Lower Austrian Government. Dear ladies and gentlemen! Interestingly, according to the opinion of the Lower Austrian Government, the NÖVOG was commissioned to develop a follow-up concept for those routes where passenger traffic is stopped. This concept was finally drawn up in consultation with the communities concerned and has now been implemented. Within the framework of this bus replacement, the transport of students is also carried out, with the Roadmap of course being coordinated with the schools. Dear ladies and gentlemen! It has now resolved everything in good pleasure: The bus replacement traffic Drösing–Zistersdorf was introduced. Finally, one last comment: Dear ladies and gentlemen! On those secondary railway lines on which passenger traffic has been stopped, the need is covered with buses. This is clear from the opinion of the Lower Austrian Government. On those routes – and this is especially important now – in which the country of Lower Austria, but also the ÖBB believe that they are interesting for tourism, there are also some projects in which, for example, nostalgia trains are used on weekends. Thus synergies are achieved for municipalities and for the region, such as the Mariazeller Bahn. Thanks to the persistence of the communities of the Pielachtal and the Pielachtal Tourism Association, it is possible to say from the current point of view that the Mariazell Railway is secured, and this is ultimately important for the future of the rural area.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00104_d7e23398	Mr President. High house! In the year before, in the year of the United Nations volunteers, I brought in a petition which was also supported by a large number of high-ranking representatives of the voluntary firearms. The aim of this petition is to restore VAT on the purchase of new equipment to the volunteer fire brigades. I think we agree that the requirements for the volunteer fire brigades are constantly rising. Each application requires a logistical and technical master achievement, and for this purpose the necessary facilities must be created and maintained. Ladies and gentlemen! Not subject to the change of time is the idealism of firemen who are willing to save, store and help at any hour. The dry figures of the statistics indicate very well the extent to which the commitment of these volunteers was and is: In total, some 5 000 Austrian fire brigades left some 200 000 deployments last year, or in other words: Every two and a half minutes, people had to be saved, animals or property protected or fire extinguished. It was around 45 000 fire operations, and another 30 000 operations had to be carried out in the event of road accidents. No fewer than 3 000 people were found. For all these uses, considerable funds are needed for the procurement of the operational vehicles and equipment, but also for their maintenance and operation. Without modern equipment, rapid and, above all, efficient assistance is simply not possible in situations where life and death are concerned. Please bear in mind: The acquisition of a tanker, such as the Lower Austrian firearm minimum equipment regulation, a national law, already prescribed for fire brigades in small communities with more than 300 houses, costs about €300 000, of which approximately €49 000 is VAT. All members of the volunteer fire brigades are only too well aware of the difficulties in financing new equipment, technical facilities and buildings. In addition to the efforts of the volunteer workers, many joint efforts are being made to raise the necessary money – events such as summer festivals, heurigens, balls, donations in the communities and the like. The funds available to fire brigade from the rise of the fire tax and the disaster fund have not been sufficient for years to cover, for example, the financial burden on fire brigades arising from the spectrum of traffic accidents. The more absurd it must be for each individual to return to the Minister for Finance as VAT every six euros taken. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! In contrast, in accordance with the 1996 Health and Social Aid Act, rescue organisations receive aid to the extent of the non-deductible advance tax. I would like to stress: That's a good thing. A similar arrangement should also be made to finance the infrastructure of firearms! Ladies and gentlemen! Instead of buying combat planes, you could buy fire brigades. These devices save lives rather than destroy human lives! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The Federation must be here for its responsibility. One cannot simply leave the volunteer fire brigades and even the communities in the financing of the necessary facilities in the rain. It is now necessary to place a concrete sign visible in the fire brigades' cash registers. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Khol.]] [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign.]] It is not enough, Mr Klubobmann Dr. Khol, to point out the importance of the volunteer fire brigades only in Sunday's speeches, as you very well know! Thank you.
